Two object sources answer the shapes the five-verb guard allowed: a **proper-noun head** (free, and it carries most of the reach, because a manager names a row by a SHORTENED title — `Repair Re-measure Gates` for *"Re-measure Gates per Finished Task After SC2-SC5…"*), and an **unquoted task title at the head**, listed LAZILY because `25 Tasks` measures 16,389 pages / 28.0 ms against 2.4 ms for topics+goals, so a menu of ordinary operator-only options never pays it. ⚠️ All three pinned operator-only shapes survive: `keep the session open` (verb not leading), `resume tomorrow` and `Open questions about Attention Board Polish` (verb leading, lowercase head). ⚠️ **A "source 6 — any head" relaxation was BUILT, MEASURED and WITHDRAWN, and the withdrawal is recorded in the file's own source so the idea is not re-proposed as untried:** it reached **0 of 41** manager-owned options allowed and broke two pre-existing pinned cases — `Resume the session — "keep going"` (pinned allowed because the quote is LATE, not the verb's object) and the `OBJECT_WINDOW` boundary (an object past 40 characters must not match). The contract was kept over the number. ⚠️ The eight options the predicate still allows are a **recorded residual** — each is a manager verb leading into a lowercase common-noun target — and the tests pin them ALLOWED so a future change that denies them is a deliberate, visible edit rather than silent drift. Measured on a 2026-10-16 corpus of 26 manager menus / 91 option labels from 8 manager sessions: manager-owned options allowed **36 → 8**, operator-only options denied **0** (the required negative control). Tests: the corpus's **33** denied options asserted **individually** so a partial widening cannot pass, the **8** residual options pinned allowed, a **colour-arm** case (the arm that carried all four live denies — a name-only guard would have caught zero), and a worker scope control on a corpus label. Change set: `scripts/manager-menu-guard.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_menu_guard.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.161.9

- fix: **a session holding TWO open attention-store items was reported as holding none, so its gate lost cross-layer de-dup and the run said the opposite of the truth.** `scripts/notify-gate.py`'s `gate_item()` joins a gate to its item on `producer_id == <the gate's subject session>` and answers `None` in **two different situations**: no item at all, and 2+ items whose tiebreak (`normalise(item["payload"]) == normalise(gate["text"])`) could not pick one. The caller read both as item-less and printed `unresolved '…' -- no open store item for session <id>` — **false on its own terms** in the second case. Measured **2026-10-16**: session `cee03b7d-0e1c-4210-a578-664b538b52df` held two open items (`389a40c754db79e474f40534ccf8f28a`, `b0a694ce8adb6409acfb684db49354ea`) while the run printed `no open store item for session cee03b7d-…`; the same store read held 14 items across 7 producers, three of them sessions carrying 2+ cards. The tiebreak is weak **by design** — the manager paraphrases the gate while `payload` is the hook's own `detail` — so it matches nothing, `None` comes back, and the gate is published **without** a stamp, leaving the other layer free to raise it again. The join is split into `gate_candidates()`; the caller classifies, and 2+ candidates report as **`ambiguous`** on a line of its own naming the session, the candidate count and their item ids. ⚠️ **`gate_item()`'s return is deliberately unchanged** — the ambiguity is *reported*, never resolved, because a tiebreak that quietly picks wrong is worse than one that reports it could not pick (`:588-590`), and stamping every candidate would silently suppress a *different* legitimate gate. ⚠️ **The de-dup loss is NOT repaired and is not claimed to be:** it stays the accepted cost of retiring the ledger (`test_notify_gate.py:774`), and both lines still state it. What is repaired is that the two causes are now separable in the feed, where before an ambiguity read as a clean item-less gate. The `unresolved` re-read now covers `ambiguous` too and **re-classifies** rather than only re-resolving, since an item closing between the two reads leaves one candidate. Change set: `scripts/notify-gate.py`, `scripts/tests/test_notify_gate.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.161.8

- fix: **the coverage gate rejected the sweep reader's own rendering, so a no-change tick fell open and dispatched every owner.** `scripts/manager-coverage-gate.py` matched `--delta` against bare tokens (`no-change` / `no change` / `unchanged` / `nochange`, and `change` / `changed`), while `commands/steps/manager-coverage.md` told the caller to pass "the sweep's own delta verdict, verbatim" and `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` renders `Delta: <what moved, or "no change">`. Measured **2026-10-16** on a live round: the rendered `Delta: no change — all 14 rows' status/phase/met/progress_hash match the frozen snapshot` line failed open and dispatched all four owners — the saving gone, silently, on exactly the tick the gate exists to make free, with every fail-open assertion still green. `normalise_delta()` now strips a leading `Delta:`, takes the leading clause, unquotes and lowercases, so the rendering the step prescribes is what the gate accepts. ⚠️ **Only the no-change rendering is normalised, deliberately:** the sweep reader renders a *change* as prose (`Delta: <what moved>`) and renders `uncomputable` in the same shape, so both stay unrecognised and fail open — deciding between them is the sweep reader's contract, not a string this gate may guess at, and guessing wrong would narrow the dispatch while the gate believed it had read the delta. Four tests added: a regression on the measured line, the rendered-token change case, a pin that a change whose prose mentions "no change" fails open rather than reading as no-change, and the `uncomputable` rendering. Change set: `scripts/manager-coverage-gate.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_coverage_gate.py`, `commands/steps/manager-coverage.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.161.7

- fix: **three instruments on the manager's gate-surfacing path each answered an existence question wrongly, and all three are repaired.** (1) **`scripts/notify-gate.py` read the attention store ONCE and never re-read it.** `items = store_items()` runs at `:817`, before the loop, and the join at `:835` scans that snapshot — so an item created after it is invisible for the whole round. The gate is then reported `unresolved` (*cross-layer de-dup is NOT in force*), published **without a stamp**, and the other layer stays free to raise it again: the de-dup loss the line exists to name. Measured **2026-10-16**: two items appeared in a 75-second window, and a gate keyed to a session the store held read `unresolved '…' -- no open store item for session 0c34ef56-…` while the same store returned item `9a2c85a3…` for that same session at the same instant. A single re-read now runs before `due` is computed, so a late item resolves and is stamped exactly as a first-pass item is — and a foreign stamp moves it to `suppressed` as it would have on the first pass. `scripts/tests/test_notify_gate.py` pairs the positive case with a negative one that differs **only** in what the second read returns, so a re-read that resolved regardless of its result passes the first and fails the second. (2) **`scripts/gate-owner-filter.py`'s `--pane` manufactured a gate instead of reading one.** `panes = [(pane, False) for pane in args.pane]` (`:1177`) built the list from the flag and never consulted the feed, so `--self beac5fd0 --pane 1832 --explain` read `gates: 1  emit: 1  reason: own-worker` while the authoritative path over the same pane read `gates: 0  emit: 0`. Because `--explain` reads as a diagnostic, a manager checking *"is this pane blocked?"* got a confident affirmative for a pane that needed nothing — the one direction that fails by **inventing** a gate rather than dropping one. `--pane` now **filters** the feed, producing it once via `--producer` when `--feed` is absent (the same refusal / failed-producer handling the poll loop uses), and `--explain` names each named pane the feed does not carry: `no feed gate for pane <N>`. Five existing tests encoded the manufacturing contract and were migrated. (3) **The absence-claim rule was narrower than the claim it guards.** It said only *"name which channel you tested"*, scoped to the feed read; it now states the general form — **a negative across N searched stores is not an absence** — and names `SendMessage` via `ListAgents` as a channel the manager holds, in all three copies (`commands/steps/manager-gates.md:11`, and the runbook's two). ⚠️ **`gate-owner-filter.py`'s emit-on-`unowned` / `liveness-unknown` path was left ALONE deliberately, and that is a finding rather than an omission:** its own docstring states at `:95-99` and again at `:152-166` that the fail-open is deliberate and that *"no predicate fix is available"*, and records this exact `emit: 1  dropped(peer-manager): 5` signature at `:140-143` as the predecessor-manager handover blindness — bounded, self-closing, with two candidate fixes already considered and rejected. Holding such a pane one poll would delay a permanent condition and turn a transient registry read failure into a dropped gate, which is the fleet-wide silence that asymmetry exists to avoid. Change set: `scripts/notify-gate.py`, `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py`, `scripts/tests/test_notify_gate.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py`, `commands/steps/manager-gates.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.161.6

- fix: **the answering arm reads the store's error code, so a card cleared on the board stops reading like one another arm already answered.** Both failures are HTTP 409, and `scripts/attention-answer.py` mapped *any* 409 onto one `LOST: … already answered or no longer open` line — the conflation that made the original diagnosis of this defect wrong. The store distinguishes them, and so does the arm now: `ITEM_CLOSED` means the card left the queue before the answer arrived (the board cleared it) and no arm can answer it; `ALREADY_ANSWERED` means another arm's verdict landed first. Each gets its own line, and the two say what the operator must do differently — the `ITEM_CLOSED` line names the card as **cleared** and records that **no verdict was recorded**, so a reader with only that line cannot mistake a bricked gate for a released one. ⚠️ **Both 409 sites are covered, not one**: `cmd_answer` (message class) and `cmd_answer_permission` (permission class) each collapsed the code, and the permission branch keeps its own `do not deliver` wording. ⚠️ **An unreadable or unknown code falls back to the generic line rather than to either named one**, on the same reasoning the spawn path refuses instead of defaulting: a body the arm cannot parse is not evidence of which failure happened, while both named lines claim a specific cause. The store carries the code in the body as `{"error":{"code":…}}` (`github.com/bborbe/http`), verified against the deployed `attention-controller` `6d57c5cd0428` (v0.54.4). `commands/attention-next.md` branches on the labels, so its answer and attempt tables are widened to match. Change set: `scripts/attention-answer.py`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_answer.py`, `commands/attention-next.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.161.5

- fix: **the manager round's coverage step (4b) now asks a gate before it dispatches, so a tick whose sweep reports no change dispatches 0 coverage agents instead of one per closure-box owner.** Measured **2026-10-16** on the Recurring Operator Gates manager (session `beac5fd0`), tick 09:05:58Z → 09:18:52Z: the sweep reported `Delta: no change` — *"all 14 rows' status/phase/met/progress_hash match the frozen snapshot"* — and step 4b dispatched **15** agents anyway (14 `vault-cli:task-manager-agent` plus 1 `vault-cli:goal-manager-agent`), at 17,516 output tokens and 770,459 counting cache reads. `scripts/manager-coverage-gate.py` owns the decision — `0` no-change, dispatch nothing / `10` change, one owner path per line and one agent per line / `2` the tracked set is unreadable, so the caller dispatches every owner — and it fails **open** on an unrecognised delta, on a CHANGE carrying no names, and on a name the tracked set cannot place: a dispatch not saved costs one subagent call, while a coverage map silently skipped on a tick that moved leaves the round reporting a stale SC-to-evidence table as current, and nothing announces the loss. ⚠️ **The delta is passed in, never recomputed** — `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` step 1 item 3 already computes it against the prior snapshot, and the step's own read-only clause forbids it becoming a second classifier. ⚠️ **`--changed` is comma- or newline-separated and never whitespace-separated**, because task names contain spaces: a whitespace split shreds every name, none resolves, the gate falls open on every call, and the saving disappears while every fail-open assertion still passes — this suite caught exactly that on its first run, which is why the separator is pinned by a regression test rather than assumed. ⚠️ **The gate belongs to the per-tick site alone.** `commands/worker-drive.md` dispatches once per run and `commands/manager-verify.md` once per verify, so neither has a repetition to suppress and neither gains a no-op gate. `scripts/tests/test_manager_coverage_gate.py` asserts the dispatch **count** — stdout lines, the caller's own dispatch list — never a clause in the step's markdown, and pairs the no-change assertion with a changed-delta positive control so a build that always answers "nothing to dispatch" cannot pass; a second clause pins that the step actually invokes the gate, because a correct gate left unwired passes every counted assertion while step 4b still dispatches on every tick. Change set: `commands/steps/manager-coverage.md`, `scripts/manager-coverage-gate.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_coverage_gate.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.161.4

- fix: **a manager no longer stands itself down a tick early when it reads its subject twice in one tick.** `scripts/manager-quiet.py` counted **invocations, not ticks** — its own docstring assumed *"a tick is one process"*, and a manager that inspected its subject twice advanced the counter twice. **That is the unsafe direction:** the rule is *fail toward ARMED*, and early is the one failure that loses work silently, since a manager left armed costs a model call while one stood down on live work stops driving it and nothing announces the loss. A tick is now identified by **`<slug>.cadence`'s mtime** — the arm the manager performs at the head of every round — and the counter records which tick it belongs to, so a **second read of the same tick is inert**. A counter written by an older version (a bare integer, with no tick key) still counts rather than being discarded; it simply cannot tell a re-read from a new tick. Found 2026-10-16 by a goal manager that read its subject twice in one tick and watched the counter go 1 → 2 → 3. ⚠️ **The tests encoded the same false assumption** — `Base.tick()` simulated a tick by calling the function, so every multi-tick case would have passed against a counter that never looked at the arm; the helper now arms first, as a real round does, and five new tests pin the distinction. Change set: `scripts/manager-quiet.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_quiet.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.161.3

- fix: **the manager drive leg's audit loop now carries a wall-clock bound, so a round with no actionable rows ends instead of running 19-35 minutes.** `agents/manager-drive.md` clause (1) dispatches one `vault-cli:task-auditor` subagent per non-terminal row, sequentially, and nothing bounded the loop — so on a round with nothing to open the audit is the *whole* of the leg's duration, and a cold tick ran to completion however long that took. Measured **2026-10-16** on the zero-actionable subject `Recurring Operator Gates Are Fixed Once, Not Answered Every Session` (its store record carries `ready-to-start → 0` and `stuck → 0`): ~34 min from `.tracked.txt` 11:05:04 to `.verdicts.json` 11:39:01, corroborated at ~19 min on `attention-controller-is-the-fleet-s-single-source-of-session-state`; `commands/steps/manager-drive.md` already carried the sibling measurement (27 dispatches, 0 cache hits, 29 minutes). `scripts/drive-audit-budget.py` owns the bound — `CONTINUE` (0) / `TERMINAL:` (3) / `REFUSED` (2), the last being the three-state discipline applied to the clock, because a caller reading a failed read as `CONTINUE` has no bound at all while every check reads green. ⚠️ **It is a wall clock and NOT a row cap, and that distinction is the whole reason it can coexist with the prohibition two paragraphs above it in the clause:** clause (1) forbids a per-tick row cap because a row cap is *silent* — it truncates coverage and reports nothing — whereas a bounded stop names the bound, counts the rows audited, and reports every row left under `Cap failure`, which is the shape that clause already blesses. `scripts/check-drive-audit-budget.py` keeps the clause wired to the script (the callee exists, the script is named twice, all three branch tokens survive, the bound's value is stated nowhere in the prose, and the row-cap prohibition is intact — the last because a reader who resolves the apparent tension by deleting the prohibition has removed the distinction the fix rests on), and `scripts/tests/test_drive_audit_budget.py` exercises the bound's own verdicts with a discrimination section requiring each mutated script to violate its own invariant. Change set: `agents/manager-drive.md`, `scripts/drive-audit-budget.py`, `scripts/check-drive-audit-budget.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_audit_budget.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_drive_audit_budget.py`, `Makefile`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.161.2

- test: **the manager-menu rule's acceptance is now a behavioural probe over a fixture subject — and building it found the canonical manager menu passing the gate.** The criterion required that no `AskUserQuestion` carry a manager-owned verb, and a text grep would have satisfied that with no behaviour change, so `scripts/manager-menu-probe.py` runs the *decision path* an `AskUserQuestion` takes — `scripts/manager-menu-guard.py`, invoked as a subprocess exactly as `hooks/hooks.json` invokes it — against a fixture subject built on disk: a temp vault whose `23 Topics` page resolves the manager's name, a registry carrying a manager and a non-manager session, and a tracked set holding one approved `phase: planning` row with no live session and one `phase: todo` row. It asserts on the exit code **and** on the record the run leaves behind, because the record is the artifact the live check reads. ⚠️ **The tracked set is READ, never decorated** — each row's own frontmatter decides which side of the line it falls on, and the probe asserts the fixture before it asserts any verdict, so a fixture that quietly stopped carrying an approved row fails here rather than passing on an empty matrix. ⚠️ **A live model turn is deliberately NOT the instrument:** it is not reproducible on demand, it would run against the real fleet, and a probe built on one could never be shown capable of going red — the property `scripts/tests/manager_menu_probe_fixture.py` establishes, by mutating the fixture three ways and requiring the probe to go red naming exactly the cases that broke, with the operator-only cases and the scope control left green. The mutations break one thing each: **the topic page renamed** so rule 1's name arm stops resolving the manager and its menus are allowed; **the record store replaced by a regular file** so the guard still exits 2 but writes nothing, which is what pins the probe to the record rather than to the exit code; and **the approved row advanced past `phase: planning`** so the fixture no longer describes the criterion. The positive control runs first — a probe that failed on everything would satisfy every red case — and `scripts/tests/test_manager_menu_probe.py` wires both into `make test`, so the falsification runs in CI by virtue of existing. ⚠️ **The probe's first run was red, and the gap was the shape a manager actually uses.** `Resume "Sentry Agent - Retire the Legacy Gate"` was ALLOWED: the predicate's object list answers only phrasings that happen to say `row` or `task`, and a manager quoting the row's own title says neither — while listing `25 Tasks` to close it would cost **51,285 files / 47.7 ms** before every `AskUserQuestion`. A third object source closes it for free: **a quoted run at the head of the window**, after an optional article, shipped with the guard and pinned by `TestQuotedTitleObjectSource`. Change set: `scripts/manager-menu-probe.py`, `scripts/tests/manager_menu_probe_fixture.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_menu_probe.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.161.1

- test: **the manager-menu guard gains its own suite — 71 tests — and the suite is where the two predicate defects are PINNED rather than merely described.** The guard shipped in the previous PR; this one brings the coverage that keeps its predicate honest. The three graded cases are all present and each is the others' control: positive (manager + manager-owned verb → deny), **negative control** (the same manager, an operator-only menu → allow) and **scope control** (the same payload from a non-manager → allow). Around them: the fail-open set (malformed stdin, a non-object payload, a missing session id, an unknown session, an unreadable registry entry, an unresolvable vault), the record (written for allow and deny alike, addressable by session id, shape pinned against a literal, ISO-8601 timestamp, both growth bounds, and a deny that survives an unwritable state dir), the colour arm's own control, and the manifest wiring with a negative control that no other event fires the guard. ⚠️ **The first predicate defect is a REQUIRED case here, not an incidental one.** The original predicate matched a manager-owned verb **anywhere** in the option label and denied two legitimate operator-only menus — `["keep the session open", "leave it unchanged"]` and `["Keep working", "resume tomorrow"]` — while its own negative control passed only because its labels happened to contain no verb at all: **a control that avoids the overlap is not a control for the overlap.** Both payloads are asserted individually, so a partial fix cannot pass. ⚠️ **The second was found by the behavioural probe that follows in the next PR:** `Resume "<bare task title>"` — the canonical manager menu — was ALLOWED, because a real task title carries no manager-owned noun. `TestQuotedTitleObjectSource` pins the quoted-head object source that closes it, alongside the two labels that must stay operator-only (`Resume the session — "keep going"`, `Open questions about "Attention Board Polish"`), which are the same false-positive class as the first defect. Change set: `scripts/tests/test_manager_menu_guard.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.161.0

- fix: **a manager no longer offers its own manager-owned actions as a menu — the rule now lives where managers read it.** Measured 2026-10-15: the Sentry Agent manager and the A2A Worker Controller manager repeatedly opened `AskUserQuestion` multi-select menus listing work that was theirs to do — auditing and opening approved rows, resuming rows with no session, repairing goal-page boxes, filing follow-up tasks — and the Sentry manager reverted to a menu within ~2 hours of being told by `SendMessage` that it should push such work forward. ⚠️ **A relayed rule cannot bind a manager, so the norm is written where it is read, at two homes:** the **always-hold invariant** in `commands/manager-loop.md` § Manager contract (a manager never presents its own manager-owned action as an `AskUserQuestion` option — execute it and report it as a table line), and the **per-round rule** in `commands/steps/manager-drive.md` § Drive, beside the clause that already calls the drive leg's `To open` / `To resume` rows *"yours to execute"*. The modal is reserved for **operator-only** decisions — a `phase: todo` approval, a production-touching or irreversible action, a TDR — and even those reach the operator as an attention-board card carrying a `--dedup-key`. ⚠️ **The rule deliberately does NOT go in `commands/manager-drive.md`,** which is where it was first proposed: that file is the **map** and says so (*"The rules do not live here, and that is deliberate… this command is the **map**"*), and it is a `WAKEUP_TARGETS` entry under a strict 10,240 B tick cap sitting at 9,847 B — **393 B of headroom**. ⚠️ **The arm's growth was landed through the budget ratchet's own prescribed remedy:** `commands/manager-loop.md` grew 26,818 → 27,905 B, which `scripts/check-budget.py` refuses by design (*"if the growth is deliberate raise the entry by hand (--tighten cannot, by design)"*), so its `scripts/budget-baseline.json` entry was raised by hand. Change set: `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/steps/manager-drive.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: **the manager-menu rule gains a gate that BLOCKS the call — a `PreToolUse` hook on `AskUserQuestion` denies a manager session whose option offers a manager-owned action.** The prose rule above did not hold on its own: measured 2026-10-15, a manager reverted to a menu **within ~2 hours** of being told by `SendMessage` that it should push the work forward, and a menu blocks its pane until the operator presses Esc. An advisory signal is what a manager ignores, so this is `exit 2` with the reason on stderr, naming the action to execute instead (`/supervisor:open "<name>"` for an approved row) rather than only the refusal. ⚠️ **The verb list is DERIVED, not invented, and is a single named constant that may not widen here:** `open` · `resume` · `ready` · `file` · `repair` is the two rule homes' own vocabulary verbatim (*"Opening or resuming an approved row, readying a row, filing a task, repairing a page box"*), grounded in `agents/manager-drive.md`'s report vocabulary (`To open` ×10, `To resume` ×8), `commands/steps/manager-repair.md` (a real step for `repairable` rows) and `commands/manager-loop.md:92` (the manager itself runs `/vault-cli:create-task`) — and the Manager Session runbook § Gate triage class A is a **closed list** (*"adding to it is an operator decision"*), so the constant carries that clause as a comment and a test pins the list against a literal. ⚠️ **Scoping is `fleet-board.py`'s rule 1 computed LOCALLY, and the board is never called:** it walks every vault and every task file at a measured **14–73 s per call (7.1 s quiet)**, and this hook runs before *every* `AskUserQuestion` — paying that would stall the very tool it polices. The local computation measures **~26 ms** (registry scan 1.3 ms · vault page names 6.9 ms · transcript glob 15.5 ms · 256 KB tail read ~2 ms). ⚠️ **BOTH arms are load-bearing, measured 2026-10-16 on the live fleet:** of the four live manager sessions, two resolve by NAME (`Sentry Agent Manager` → topic `Sentry Agent`; `Fleet Manager` → the root name) and two resolve only by COLOUR (`Single Source of Session State Manager`, `⚙ A2A Worker Controller Manager` — neither names a topic or goal page), so a name-only guard would miss half the fleet's managers. The colour arm reads the session's **own transcript** because the registry carries no colour key — `fleet-colours.py`'s header says so, and colour is persisted only as the last `{"type":"agent-color",…}` record — as a **bounded backwards scan from the tail** (256 KB windows, 8 MB cap) rather than a full read, since transcripts reach **500 MB** (median 3 MB over 5,753 files); all 18 live sessions resolved inside the first window. ⚠️ **`gate-owner-filter.py`'s `is_manager(session_id, ledger)` is a DIFFERENT rule** (*"a session with no ledger record reads AS a manager"*) and is deliberately not imported, mirrored or blended — a session with no ledger record is the common case here, so blending it would deny the menus of workers, the scope that must keep working. ⚠️ **Fail-open on every unproven path** — malformed stdin, a missing `session_id`, an unreadable registry entry, an unresolvable vault, a non-manager session — because a gate that failed closed on a parse error would block every `AskUserQuestion` in every session that loads this plugin; the session id comes from stdin's `session_id` with `$CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` as the fallback and **never `$$`**, which names no session. Kill switch: `SUPERVISOR_MANAGER_MENU_GUARD=off`. ⚠️ **The predicate is "does this option NAME a manager-owned action", and the first one — a bare word-boundary match on the label — was measured wrong.** It denied two legitimate operator-only menus against the live `Sentry Agent Manager` session (2026-10-16): `["keep the session open", "leave it unchanged"]` (on `open`) and `["Keep working", "resume tomorrow"]` (on `resume`) — both standard operator-only option shapes, and the original negative control passed only because its labels happened to contain no verb at all. An option now denies only when a manager-owned verb **leads** it (after stripping the enumerator, bullet or backtick a menu adds) **and** a manager-owned **object** follows within 40 characters — one of `row` / `task` / `page` / `box` / `goal`, or one of the vault's own topic and goal page titles at the head of the window. ⚠️ **Both halves are load-bearing and each alone is a defect:** leading-only denies `"Resume tomorrow"`, object-only denies `"keep the session open"`; both regression payloads are required cases in the test file, asserted individually so a partial fix cannot pass. ⚠️ **Named residuals, all in the allow direction:** a bare task title with no object noun **and no quoting** (`Open A Docker Spawn's Create Reply …`) — reading `25 Tasks` here would cost **51,285 files / 47.7 ms** before every `AskUserQuestion`, and the quoted-head object source added below covers the form a manager actually uses instead; a manager-owned verb with no object (`"Open"`); and a verb that is not leading (`"Do not open the row"`, a legitimate decline). Option `description` text is not scanned — only the label, which is the choice being offered. ⚠️ **Every invocation now writes a per-invocation RECORD, which is what makes a denial provable rather than merely claimed.** One JSON line per invocation, appended to `~/.claude/state/manager-menu-guard/<session_id>.jsonl` (`SUPERVISOR_MANAGER_MENU_GUARD_STATE_DIR` overrides the directory; a session with no readable id lands in `unknown-session.jsonl`), with a fixed shape a reader can depend on: `ts` (ISO-8601, UTC) · `session_id` · `decision` (`allow`/`deny`) · `reason` (`manager-owned-action` / `no-manager-action` / `non-manager` / `kill-switch` / `fail-open: <cause>`) · `manager` (`true`/`false`/`null` — `null` meaning the verdict was never evaluated, a different answer from "evaluated, and it was false") · `manager_arm` (`root`/`name`/`colour`, so a name-detected manager is distinguishable from the colour-only ones) · `verbs`. ⚠️ **Written on ALLOW and DENY alike, and on the kill-switch and fail-open paths too** — a guard that recorded only its denies could not be told from one that never fired, which is the defect this whole change exists to remove one layer up. ⚠️ **The write is fail-open and can never change the verdict** — it runs after the decision is fixed, every failure inside it is swallowed, and its result is never consulted by the decision path, so an unwritable directory or a failed append leaves the hook exiting with the code it computed; **a lost record is preferred to a wrong verdict**. ⚠️ **Growth is bounded by a trim, not by hope:** a session's file is trimmed to its most recent 200 lines once it passes either bound (256 KB, or 200 lines — the line count is what actually binds at the measured ~190 B per record), written through a temporary file and `os.replace`d so a kill mid-trim cannot leave a partial file a reader would mistake for a record that was never written. The manifest gains its **first `PreToolUse` group**, carrying `"matcher": "AskUserQuestion"` so the guard does not spawn on every tool call, with its purpose, fail-open direction, record path/shape/bound and kill switch in the top-level `description` per this repo's convention. ⚠️ **The guard's own test suite and the behavioural probe ship in two follow-up PRs** — this change is split three ways because the auto-reviewer parks any diff over 1200 added lines. The graded cases — positive, **negative control** (the same manager, an operator-only menu, allowed), **scope control** (the same payload from a non-manager, allowed) — plus the two overlap regression payloads, the five verbs in their natural manager-owned phrasing, the predicate's boundaries, the fail-open set, the record (written for allow and deny alike, addressable by session id, shape pinned against a literal, ISO-8601 timestamp, both bounds, and a deny that survives an unwritable state dir), the colour arm's own control, the manifest wiring with a negative control that no other event fires the guard, and a pin that the mirrored `_norm_session_name` still agrees with the canonical copy in `scripts/fleet-board.py`. Change set: `scripts/manager-menu-guard.py`, `hooks/hooks.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.160.0

- feat: **the fleet sweep's classification table now carries `stood-down` and `lapsed`, so a manager that stood itself down is no longer rendered as a fault.** `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`'s step-8 table gains the row `STOPPED` → **`stood-down`** (a *correct* self-stand-down, never nudged) · `STALE` → **`lapsed`** (cadence expired with no `<slug>.stopped` marker, so nothing stood it down), and the `CLASSIFICATION` line gains both terms — it must stay arithmetic, so each new class has to reconcile with the rows printed beneath it. ⚠️ **The row is ranked above `stalled` and `parked` deliberately:** a stood-down manager is idle with a frozen cadence and an untouched task file, so every signal below reads it as a fault and a manager reading one is invited to nudge **by hand** — the exact act the stand-down exists to prevent. `lapsed` is the opposite and is deliberately not folded in with it: no marker means the loop simply stopped, which is what this mechanism exists to surface. ⚠️ **Getting the row in cost prose, and the deletions are named rather than silent:** the file sits against a **30720-byte body budget** and `scripts/check-budget.py` refuses a new oversized body outright rather than ratcheting it, so the row was funded by cutting a duplicated restatement, a justification clause and a rule pointer — **no rule was removed**, and the precedence detail moved into `docs/sweep-output.md`, which says what each class means and why the ranking holds. Two fixture tests pin it: one builds a stood-down and a lapsed manager from the same frozen cadence and asserts they classify differently, the other pins the shipped table's mapping so the two labels cannot be swapped. Change set: `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `docs/sweep-output.md`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_liveness.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.159.0

- feat: **the fleet sweep now tells a stood-down manager from a lapsed one, and stops calling a correct stand-down a fault.** `scripts/manager-liveness.py` gains `--state --topic <T>`, printing `<STATE>\t<slug>\t<detail>` and exiting **0 whatever the state** — the state *is* the answer, and a non-zero exit would make a stood-down manager read as a failure to anything wrapping the call. ⚠️ **`--check` structurally could not answer this, which is why this is a new verb rather than a new flag:** it prints only the topics that *turned* stale, so `STOPPED` — the state a deliberate stand-down produces — never appears in its output at all, and a sweep that asked it, saw nothing and concluded the manager was fine has it exactly backwards. One test pins that property, so a later change cannot quietly make the two agree and hide the distinction. `docs/sweep-output.md` documents the two classes — `STOPPED` → `⏸️ stood-down` (a **correct** stand-down, ranked **above** `stalled` and `parked` because a stood-down manager is idle with a frozen cadence and an untouched task file, so every signal below reads it as a fault and invites a **hand** nudge, the exact act the stand-down exists to prevent) and `STALE` → `⚠️ lapsed` (the cadence expired with **no** `<slug>.stopped` marker, so nothing stood it down and the loop simply stopped) — as **classification classes reusing `⏸️` and `⚠️`, adding no eighth icon**, and explicitly **not** in the Session cell, where the `live` / `parked` words were retired. ⚠️ **They are documented there rather than in `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, whose classification table emits them, because that file sits at 30713 bytes against a 30720 body budget** and `scripts/check-budget.py` refuses a new oversized body outright rather than ratcheting it — the rows could not be added there without deleting existing normative prose to fund them, and the spec records that cause so a reader does not read it as an oversight. Change set: `scripts/manager-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_liveness.py`, `docs/sweep-output.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.158.0

- feat: **a subject manager now checks, every tick, whether its subject has anything left to drive — and stands itself down on the second consecutive quiet tick.** New `commands/steps/manager-quiet.md` (router row `2b`, between the readings and the sweep) runs `scripts/manager-quiet.py` for the round's subject. On `stood-down` the round **ends** — no sweep, no drive leg, no spawn — and the disarm runs through the `/supervisor:stop` contract in `commands/stop.md`, which stays the one home for what must be disarmed, which surface reaches each driver, and what must be left running. ⚠️ **Two things the step owns, because they differ from the operator running `stop` by hand:** the `<slug>.stopped` marker is written **before** the report rather than after it, so a deliberate stand-down is not later reported as a lapsed manager; and the round's last output is the script's own line — `Stood down: zero runnable tasks; reopen on <trigger>` — printed **verbatim**, because the trigger is computed from the *same read that produced the verdict* and recomputing it in the step could name a different date than the one the stand-down was decided on. ⚠️ **The trigger names its content, never merely the words `reopen on`** (an empty tail would satisfy the literal probe while naming nothing): the earliest future `defer_date` when a quiet row carries one, otherwise the **blockers** by name — never the blocked rows, since the subject reopens when a *blocker* completes and naming the waiting row points a reader at a task that is fine — otherwise the literal `no named trigger`. ⚠️ **Never `ScheduleWakeup {stop:true}` to disarm:** measured 2026-09-30 it returned *"there was no pending wakeup to cancel"* while `CronList` showed the job still armed and firing. ⚠️ **The frozen-tree rule beside it is a different predicate, not a second spelling of this one** — that one fires on no tracked file's mtime moving for ≥2 intervals, this one on zero runnable tasks; a subject can satisfy either without the other, and neither may be read as evidence for the other. Change set: `commands/steps/manager-quiet.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md`, `scripts/manager-quiet.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_quiet.py`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.157.0

- feat: **the `cluster` spawn target is RETIRED — `spawn_agent(target: "cluster", …)` now refuses, and `k8s` is the route to a worker in that cluster.** The shared long-lived `claude-interactive` pod in nuke dev was held behind a named precondition in the goal's own `# Non-goals` (*"retirement is a follow-up once SC3 is proven"*, operator decision 2026-10-13), and SC3 was met 2026-10-14: a per-task k3s pod carried a real vault task to done over two A2A turns on one `contextId`, then `stop` removed the pod and its `Config` CR. A usage read over four pinned roots — the source tree, the live config, every MCP declaration, and the **spawn ledger** — found no caller: the only live trace was `cluster.url` in `~/.config/claude-supervisor/config.json`, which no spawn path consumes. ⚠️ **THE RETIREMENT IS THREE EDITS, NOT TWO, and the third is the one a caller reads.** `server/spawn-mode.mjs`'s `SPAWN_TARGETS` and `IMPLEMENTED_SPAWN_TARGETS` lose the entry, but `server/supervisor.mjs`'s `spawn_agent` tool schema carries its own **hardcoded** `enum` and imports only `A2A_SPAWN_TARGETS` — never `SPAWN_TARGETS` — so a two-list deletion would have shipped red tests, a refusal string still advertising `target: "cluster"`, and a published tool still OFFERING a target the server refuses. A new source-level assertion pins the schema's enum against `SPAWN_TARGETS` so the two cannot drift again (verified to fire on exactly that defect), because nothing in the suite read the surface a caller actually sees. ⚠️ **A coupling worth knowing before you probe it: with `cluster` gone from the enum, Claude Code rejects the call CLIENT-SIDE and it never reaches the server — so no server-side refusal string is obtainable from a live call, and the refusal's wording is carried by a unit test on the pure `resolveSpawnTarget` instead.** The live half of the verification is now the schema-level rejection plus `list_agents` showing no new worker. ⚠️ **The TARGET is retired, not the BACKEND:** `server/cluster-spawn.mjs`, the `status: 'cluster'` roster value, the cluster arm of `message-channel.mjs` and `cluster-heartbeat.mjs` all stay, because workers spawned BEFORE this change are still running and still need their follow-up channel and their liveness stamp; the `cluster` dispatch branch in `supervisor.mjs` is left in place and is now unreachable from the list. Docs reconciled in lockstep: `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 7, `README.md` § Configuration and § Tools. Change set: `server/spawn-mode.mjs`, `server/spawn-mode.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.156.0

- feat: **a subject's zero-runnable state is now a counter on disk rather than a judgement call.** New `scripts/manager-quiet.py` records one tick for a subject and reports `armed` or `stood-down`: a tick is quiet when every non-terminal tracked task is unsurfaced (`next` / `backlog` / `hold`), deferred past today, or blocked by an unmet `blocked_by`, and the stand-down is reached on the **second consecutive** quiet tick. Measured 2026-10-15: two managers (goal `Recurring Operator Gates Are Fixed Once, Not Answered Every Session`, goal `Drive Rounds Report Success-Criterion Progress, Not Session Activity`) each carried a hand-written `<slug>.stopped` marker at the 900 s default cadence, because nothing in the loop could tell "this subject is quiet" from "this tick had no news". ⚠️ **It fails toward ARMED, never toward stood-down** — a task this check cannot prove quiet counts as runnable and resets the counter, because a manager left armed on a quiet subject wastes one model call while a manager stood down on live work silently stops driving it and nothing announces the loss; so an unreadable status, an unparseable date and an unresolved blocker all read as *work may remain*. ⚠️ **`clock-bound` is only as mechanical as `defer_date`.** The criterion names three quiet shapes and the first two are frontmatter reads; the third is prose, and no reader here parses prose into a date, so it is covered only where the author also set `defer_date` (the vault's own convention for exactly this) and elsewhere the row stays runnable. ⚠️ The counter is `<slug>.quiet` in `~/.claude/state/sweep-gate/`, beside the `<slug>.cadence` / `.arms` / `.stopped` files `manager-liveness.py` already owns and under the **same** `slug()`, so a stand-down cannot be recorded under a name no other reader resolves — pinned by a test that compares this script's slug against `manager-liveness.py`'s for three real subject names. The readers are `manager-predispatch.py`'s (`resolve_subject`, `resolve_tracked`, `blocked_by_verdict`, `split_frontmatter`), loaded by path rather than re-implemented, so this check and the sweep cannot come to disagree about what a task is. Change set: `scripts/manager-quiet.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_quiet.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.155.2

- refactor: `/supervisor:worker-drive`'s §0b diagnosis and §1 drive table move into a new read-only agent, `supervisor:worker-drive-reader`. The command re-injected 22,483 B of inline reading instructions on every run and none of it earned anything by sitting inline — the raw material (a session digest, a wait list, process checks, a coverage table) is worthless once classified, so it now stays in the reader's context and only the classification comes back. `commands/worker-drive.md` drops **61,601 → 43,583 B**, and `scripts/budget-baseline.json`'s entry is tightened to match — the ratchet moves **down** for the first time. ⚠️ **Three writes stay in the command, deliberately:** §0b's diagnose snapshot, §1's live `TodoWrite`, and §1's peer-safety-gated `# Progress` append. A subagent's `TodoWrite` does not render in the operator's UI — the whole point of that step — and the reader holds neither this session's `claude_session_id` nor its UI, so relocating them would have silently removed the operator's live progress view and the peer-safety gate. ⚠️ **The reader consumes the coverage dispatch rather than re-deriving it:** it has no `Task` grant by design, so `/supervisor:worker-drive` still dispatches `vault-cli:task-manager-agent` with `Action: coverage` and passes the result in as one of seven explicit inputs. The reader cannot see the parent conversation, so `session_digest` and `waits` must be passed explicitly — a fact not passed comes back `UNKNOWN`, which reads as a dead wait. Change set: `commands/worker-drive.md`, `agents/worker-drive-reader.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `llms.txt`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.155.1

- fix: a spawn for a task that already has a worker now reaps that worker and retries the create once, instead of failing outright. ⚠️ The controller refuses a repeat `create` for a task that already has a worker, and its refusal names that worker — but a backend failure reaches this side as a bare `TASK_STATE_FAILED` with **no detail attached**, so the refusal is indistinguishable from a transport failure by its text and the id it names never arrives. `list` is what tells the two apart: it is the only client-side read that answers whether this task already has a worker, and when it does, that worker is what the create was refused over. ⚠️ **`none` is not a failure** — a create that failed for its own reasons (an unreachable controller, a rejected token) has no worker to reap, and the ORIGINAL error is reported rather than a reaping failure invented for it. ⚠️ The retry is **bounded to one**: a second create for a task whose worker was just stopped either succeeds or fails for a reason a third attempt cannot fix, and an unbounded loop would mint and reap workers forever against a controller that keeps refusing. New `reapExistingWorkerForTask` in `server/a2a-spawn.mjs`, called from `spawnA2AWorker`. Change set: `server/a2a-spawn.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `server/a2a-spawn.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.155.0

- feat: **`/supervisor:worker-drive` now closes with one modal — the run's only ask.** v0.154.0 wired the coverage map into all three tiers but left §8 reporting without asking, so a run's decisions stayed scattered through the prose and the operator was handed a reading task. §8 now ends with exactly ONE `AskUserQuestion`: at most **4 options**, each **reversible**, each carrying the recommended pick, and **every pick executed in the same turn**. ⚠️ **Production-touching, irreversible and criterion-rewording items are excluded from it** — they stay separate `approve:` lines on a self-posted card, because a modal is a multi-select over cheap reversible work and a blast radius is not a checkbox. ⚠️ **This is the site the parent task's probe looks for, and the reason this commit exists:** `grep -rn 'AskUserQuestion' commands/ agents/`, minus the frontmatter `allowed-tools` declarations and the negation-filtered prose, must return the pre-change set **plus exactly this one** — and at v0.154.0 it returned none, which is what caught the omission. ⚠️ **The manager tier deliberately does not take the same shape.** The 2026-10-15 measurement recorded in `v0.154.0` showed a scheduled wakeup cannot reach a round that is mid-turn, so a manager round parked on a modal stalls its own next tick; that tier keeps the attention-board card path (`commands/steps/manager-gates.md`), and `commands/steps/escalate.md` is fleet-owned and out of this change's scope. ⚠️ **The `commands/worker-drive.md` ratchet is raised again by hand, 59807 → 61601**, for the same deliberate-growth reason as the entry below. Change set: `commands/worker-drive.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.154.0

- feat: **a drive round now reports which Success Criterion moved, not only which session is busy.** Every tier rebuilt its own SC → task → evidence map by hand and none of them reported one: `commands/worker-drive.md` §1's table (`# | Open item | Blocked by | Unlock lever | Owner | Now?`) carried no evidence column for met-looking boxes, `commands/manager-drive.md`'s round had no coverage step at all, and `agents/manager-verify.md` check 3 read `GAP` by hand because no instrument covered it. All three now consume the one implementation — vault-cli's `task-manager-agent` / `goal-manager-agent` `Action: coverage`, whose row shape is `<box> | <driving subtask or none> | <evidence: measured(<source>) | asserted | none> | <proposal>`. ⚠️ **The dispatch is the caller's act, never the verify agent's** — `agents/manager-verify.md` holds no `Task` grant (`:5`, `tools: Read, Bash`) and its own contract at `:69` states that outright, so the third site is `commands/manager-verify.md` step 4, and check 3 was renamed `COVERAGE` to read what the caller passes it rather than to dispatch for itself. ⚠️ **The router row is `4b`, a stable label rather than a renumbering** — rows 6 and 7 are referenced *by number* from other step files, so inserting a step in sequence would silently repoint those references at the wrong step. ⚠️ **Both ratchet entries are raised by hand, and the growth is the feature:** `commands/manager-drive.md` 9458 → 9847 and `commands/worker-drive.md` 58138 → 59807, the remedy `check-budget.py` names for deliberate growth. ⚠️ **And the measurement that shaped it, taken 2026-10-15:** three one-shot crons armed at 18:36 / 18:37 / 18:38 with `AskUserQuestion` modals open across them did not fire; the two that survived fired at 18:39:39 — the instant the turn ended — deferred 3m39s and 1m39s past their due times. A scheduled wakeup cannot reach a round that is mid-turn, so a round that parks on a modal stalls its own next tick; the wakeup rounds therefore keep the card path and were deliberately **not** given a modal. Change set: `commands/worker-drive.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md`, `commands/manager-verify.md`, `agents/manager-verify.md`, `commands/steps/manager-coverage.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.153.1

- fix: **the four route-known `/vault-cli:create-task` callers in this repo now pass the route flag, so `create-task` stops asking a question the caller already knew the answer to.** `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/steps/act.md`, `commands/steps/manager-spawn.md` and `docs/fleet-surface.md` each invoked `/vault-cli:create-task` bare, and `create-task` answers a missing `--docs-only`/`--code` with an `AskUserQuestion` in interactive mode. Measured 2026-10-15: the question *"Docs-only change (guide / KB page / runbook / note)?"* was asked in **7 sessions** over the preceding 7 days (`/recurring-gates 7`, key `ask docs-only change guide`). Every one of these four sites authors the task it files, so it knows whether the change is docs-only; each now passes the flag it knows, reserving the question for a caller that genuinely does not. The other six `/vault-cli:create-task` dispatch sites were left bare on purpose — they take free-text input and cannot derive the route, and `create-task`'s non-interactive mode deliberately refuses to guess. ⚠️ **The 116-byte growth of `commands/manager-loop.md` is deliberate, not fat**, so its `scripts/budget-baseline.json` ratchet entry is raised by hand to 26818 — the remedy `check-budget.py` names for growth that is not padding, and the only one of the four edited bodies the ratchet holds. Change set: `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/steps/act.md`, `commands/steps/manager-spawn.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.153.0

- fix: **the `## Unreleased` section is restored after a merge folded its entry into the already-cut `v0.152.4`.** PR #331 merged at 16:34 after the release bot had cut `v0.152.4` at 16:03 from a master whose `## Unreleased` held only PR #332's fix; GitHub merged the branch into master-at-`f9029744`, and the three-way merge placed the docker bullet **inside** the released section while dropping the `## Unreleased` heading. ⚠️ **The tag settles which way to repair, not the merge:** `git tag --contains 0964d19c` returns nothing while `git tag --contains f9029744` returns `v0.152.4`, so the work is in no tag and the bullet moves back out. The consequence of leaving it was concrete rather than cosmetic — with no `## Unreleased` left, the release watcher had nothing to cut, so the change would never have been released at all. Change set: `CHANGELOG.md`.

- feat: **the `docker` spawn target now resolves its own control token, so `spawn_agent(target: "docker", …)` no longer refuses by name.** `scripts/mcp-start.py` `TOKEN_KEYS` carried the k8s entry only, which made a docker spawn fail before any request was sent — the intended failure while no local controller existed, and a gap once one did. The entry (`SUPERVISOR_A2A_TOKEN_DOCKER`, resolved from the local controller's own TeamVault key) now sits beside the k8s one, keeping the one-token-per-target rule intact: the two controllers are separate deployments, so a shared variable would force both to carry the same secret. ⚠️ **The comment above the tuple said the docker secret did not exist because that controller was not deployed; both halves had become false and it is rewritten rather than left describing behaviour that had changed.** The four test assertions that pin the declared key set are updated with it — `VALUES`, `sorted(seen)`, `sorted(declared)`, and the two degrade tests, the last of which needed the new key added to its `failing` list to keep asserting a *partial* failure rather than a total one. Change set: `scripts/mcp-start.py`, `scripts/tests/test_mcp_start.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.152.4

- fix: **a finished headless worker stops reading live, because the self-stamp now stands down for a session another writer owns.** One session id had TWO writers: the server that spawned a headless worker stamps it every 30 s (`mode: 'headless'`) and CLEARS that stamp when the turn ends (`agent-loop.mjs`'s `finally`), while the worker's own session — which inherits the `supervisor` MCP server from its launcher — started a server that ran the self-stamp on the SAME file, keyed on the session id alone (`stampPath`). The last writer won, and the self-stamp outlived the worker: measured 2026-10-15 on session `4d0f1d11`, the ledger read `status: done` with `ended_at 12:14:50.538Z` while the self-stamp re-created the deleted file at 12:15:10 and again at 12:15:40, so `checkLiveness`, the store's own endpoint and an operator were all told a finished worker was still running — which is how the `SC3` probe on the task "Prove the Spawn Ledger End to End on the Live Fleet" read `live: true` 21.6 s after a peer manager had correctly omitted the row. ⚠️ **The `mode` discriminator could not be the repair, and this is the part worth keeping:** the self-stamp wrote `local` over `headless` within one tick, so a reader filtering `mode === 'headless'` could not find a live headless worker AT ALL — measured on this host, of 769 stamps every recent headless worker read `mode: local, source: mcp-timer` and the only six `mode: headless` rows left were from 2026-09-29 and 2026-10-06, i.e. written before the self-stamp existed. A merge, a second filename or a mode-preserving write all leave the timer free to write AFTER the owner's clear, which is the defect itself; only a self-stamp that never writes survives it. So `startSelfStamp` now returns `null` — no file, no timer — when the server's own `SUPERVISOR_WORKER_MODE` is `headless` (`selfStampRefusal`), the value `workerEnvFor` hands the worker on both the SDK and the tab path and the one `scripts/permission-answer-poll.py` already gates on. ⚠️ **`headless` ONLY, and the narrowness is load-bearing:** a tab worker's session is stamped by nobody else — `heartbeat.start` is reached from `agent-loop.mjs` alone — so standing down there would make every tab worker read dead, and an ABSENT value is not "a worker" but the operator's own session or a path-B resume that goes through no server, which is the case the self-stamp exists for. ⚠️ **The standing-down is LOGGED** (`supervisor.mjs`'s `startSelf`), because "no self-stamp" and "a stamp that failed to write" are otherwise indistinguishable. ⚠️ **One field is lost and no reader consumes it:** a headless worker's row no longer carries the self-stamp's `activity`, and `grep -rn '"activity"' scripts/ server/` returns nothing — the attention-controller store itself notes *"nothing in-repo consumes State beyond serialization"*. `mode` and the row's liveness are unchanged for every other kind of session. Tests: three cases driving the real ORDER rather than the functions — owner stamps → the turn ends and the owner clears → the self-stamp's next tick → read — plus the stand-down's no-file assertion; all three FAIL against the pre-fix behaviour (23 pass / 3 fail) and pass here (26/26). Change set: `server/heartbeat.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `server/config.mjs`, `server/heartbeat.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.152.3

- fix: **a pod's attention card now defaults to `owner:` liveness and carries an `expires_at`, so a gate survives long enough to be answered instead of being pruned before the operator ever sees it.** `pod-attention.py` defaulted `liveness_ref` to `session:<producer-id>`, and the store resolves `session:` against its **own host's** registry `~/.claude/sessions` (`attention-controller` `pkg/session-liveness-checker.go`: `IsLive`, and `list`, which reads that one directory and nothing else) — a directory a container's session id can never appear in, as this script's own docstring already said (*"A pod has neither a registry entry nor a spawner"*). `IsLive` therefore answered false, and the store's read path pruned the card as a dead asker's (`pkg/attention-store-impl.go` `classifyForRead`: `isAsked` + `!live` → `remove`) on the very first list read: accepted with a 201, deleted before anyone saw it, and `cmd_gate` then polled a 404 until its 900 s deadline and exited `NO_DECISION:` — the silent stall this fixes. `owner:` is the model built for exactly this case (`isProducerLiveWith` returns true for it unconditionally and deliberately, because a human owner's absence cannot be read from the registry — a never-registered owner and an exited one produce the same signal), and it is what `attention-ask.py` already moved its own default to. ⚠️ **The default and the bound are ONE change, and each half fails alone:** `owner:` is exempt from the liveness prune by design, so `expires_at` is the only thing that ever removes the card — `owner:` without a bound would trade "the card vanishes before it is read" for "a dead pod's card is immortal", and this script set no `expires_at` at all. The `gate` arm bounds its card by its own `--timeout`, so it lives exactly as long as the pod is willing to wait; the `ask` arm uses a TTL, mirroring `attention-ask.py`'s `DEFAULT_ASK_TTL_HOURS`. Both arms accept an explicit `--expires-at`, refused when unparseable, offset-less or already past (a past deadline is swept on the first read — this change's own defect, reachable with no clock skew). The rationale comment that kept `session:` here — *"a pod's `gate` blocks on its own item, so the pod is alive for as long as the gate matters"* — is kept in place and answered rather than deleted: every clause of it is true about the pod and false about the store, because the premise required the store to observe the pod and it cannot. Change set: `scripts/pod-attention.py`, `scripts/tests/test_pod_attention.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.152.2

- fix: **the `role: human` / `role: manager` carve-out is phase-INDEPENDENT, so a sessionless role'd row at `phase: execution` renders `👤 YOURS` instead of `🔄 progressing` or `⚠️ problem`.** This file scoped the carve-out to the `ready-to-start` branch — *"applies only where `ready-to-start` would otherwise win"* — and the vault's model-free gate scoped its role test the same way, inside the arm reached only by `status: next` and `in_progress` at `planning`. A `role: human` row at `phase: execution` therefore reached neither test: it rendered `🔄 progressing`, asserting work is happening with nobody to do it, or `⚠️ problem` via the `stuck` bullet, which prescribes a nudge with no target. Measured 2026-10-15 on a live row (`role: human`, `phase: execution`, 11 open boxes, no session): `🔄 progressing` at 08:26, `⚠️ problem` at 09:13. ⚠️ **Three things still WIN over the carve-out, and the ordering is load-bearing:** the terminal statuses keep their own cells; `blocked-upstream` wins, because a role'd task that is genuinely blocked is named better as blocked than as merely yours; and a **live owner** wins, because that row *is* being worked — the carve-out is for the **OWNERLESS** row. ⚠️ **And the ownerless case must not fall to the orphan candidate either**, which is exactly what the worked example was: the carve-out claims the row on the *role*, before any liveness or orphan question is asked, so the `progressing` bullet's ownerless rule now names the carve-out as its one exception. ⚠️ **What is suppressed is the spawn offer and the nudge — never the `👤 YOURS: <task> — not a spawn target` line**, which is unchanged; suppressing it would reintroduce the two-renderer divergence in the other direction. The vault's gate and runbook changed in lockstep, and its fixture gains six role cases (three at `phase: execution`, plus LIVE-owner, blocked and roleless-stuck controls) because a branch placed anywhere in `_classify()` satisfies the positives and only the controls separate "carve out the role'd row" from "swallow the bucket set". Change set: `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.152.1

- fix: **a worker parked on an operator gate is no longer read as `not parked` when a background child holds a shell open.** `scripts/who-needs-my-help.py::parked()` bucketed every status that was neither `waiting` nor `idle` as "not parked" *before* it read the closer, and `shell` is exactly that status. ⚠️ **`shell` is a shell HELD, not a turn running:** the Claude Code registry stamps it when a shell is spawned — a `run_in_background` child (a dark-factory daemon, a watcher, a `sleep`) holds one for the child's whole life — and never resets it to `idle` at turn end. Measured 2026-10-15 over a 23-session registry: every one of the six `shell` sessions held a long-lived shell child and no session without one read `shell`; pane 1642's worker had read `shell` for 9h27m from the moment it backgrounded its own daemon, its turn already ended on an operator gate, while `who-needs-my-help.py` rendered `not parked (11): … [1642] ⚙ Vault UI Board Spawns ps Every Minute While Idle …` — an open production gate invisible to the sweep the operator reads. ⚠️ **The limb is not `idle`'s rule under a second name, and the two extra clauses are what keep a mid-turn session out:** a session genuinely mid-turn also reads `shell` for the duration of any Bash call, and its last assistant text is then the *previous* turn's closer — so the limb requires (a) no user or tool turn after the closer, the shipped `turn_after_closer()` read, and (b) a closer read from a `👤 You:` line via `closer_from_transcript()`, never the arbitrary-prose `detail` fallback inside `current_closer()`, admitted only for an operator verb or a `later (on <trigger>):` machine-bound park. Clause (b) is not decoration: panes 1557 and 1619 both read as gates off prose on 2026-10-15 while all three instruments disagreed. `idle` keeps the broader rule deliberately — it is only reached with the turn provably over, so a machine-bound park must still count. ⚠️ **`agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` step 8 gains the matching row, placed above `stalled`,** because both rows below it misfire on this shape: `busy`/`shell` with an unchanged mtime reads **stalled**, and the `idle`-gated park row never sees it — so the digest rendered an open gate as a problem row and invited a nudge at a worker that was correctly waiting. ⚠️ **`scripts/manager-attention-watch.py::is_gated()` is deliberately NOT widened here.** Its `is_ask()` accepts any non-`nothing` closer, so the same limb there would gate on prose, and it carries no turn-position read to keep a mid-turn session out; the safe version needs `is_operator_ask()` to move to the shared `who-needs-me.py` rather than be restated in a second script — the drift this repo forbids. Tests: `scripts/tests/test_who_needs_my_help.py` gains `ParkedWithShellTest`, six cases pinning one clause each (operator ask, machine-bound wait, a turn taken after the closer, prose, `nothing`, `busy`); that suite reads **61**. Change set: `scripts/who-needs-my-help.py`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `scripts/tests/test_who_needs_my_help.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.152.0

- feat: **a spawn target now resolves its OWN controller — per-target URL and token, so `target: "docker"` can no longer reach the k8s controller.** Measured 2026-10-14: a `spawn_agent(target: "docker", …)` returned `target: "docker"` while the controller created a **k8s pod**, and the `stop_agent` reply for the same worker said `"backend": "k8s"`. The cause was structural rather than a slip — the create request carried **no target field**, and one `a2a.url` served both targets, so the controller's own `-backend` flag decided what ran and the supervisor's label could disagree with it. ⚠️ **A single URL was never a valid default**, which is why the old shape is REFUSED rather than read: the docker backend shells out to `docker` on whichever host runs it (`NewDockerCLIClient`) and the k8s backend needs cluster credentials, so one address is wrong for one target whichever one it holds — a docker-target spawn against the nuke-dev ingress creates a container *inside the pod*, not on this Mac. `a2a.url` is now a target-keyed map (`{"docker": …, "k8s": …}`), the environment is `SUPERVISOR_A2A_URL_<TARGET>` / `SUPERVISOR_A2A_TOKEN_<TARGET>`, and `resolveA2AControllerTarget` takes a required `target` and refuses an unknown one. ⚠️ **The liveness poll now fans out over every configured target.** It resolved one controller and listed one worker set; with two controllers behind two URLs a single list call can only see half the fleet, and because `pollA2A` stamps what it lists and never un-stamps what it does not, the other half would have gone unstamped and read as dead while running. Fanning out is safe for exactly that reason, and a target that is unreachable still contributes `ok: false` rather than a false "no workers". ⚠️ **Migration:** the single `SUPERVISOR_A2A_URL` / `SUPERVISOR_A2A_TOKEN` and a bare-string `a2a.url` are refused with a message naming the replacement. The docker controller's own control secret does not exist yet — that controller is not deployed — so a `docker` spawn refuses by name until its entry is added to `TOKEN_KEYS` in `scripts/mcp-start.py`.

## v0.151.3

- fix: **`/supervisor:who-needs-my-help` reads a manager's next SC/DoD item off the page it is anchored to, so the `Managers to query` block carries content for every manager instead of an empty `next:` line.** A manager session is anchored to a goal or topic PAGE, never to a task file, so `next_item()` — which read the anchored task file — returned nothing for all four live managers when measured 2026-10-15. For a session `is_manager()` accepts the sweep now resolves the subject through source 1 of `docs/subject-resolution.md` (`~/.claude/state/worker-manager/<session-id>.json`) and reads the page that subject names; anything else keeps the task-file read. A subject that no longer resolves is a silent miss that falls back to the task file, never an error, and the `vault` value is compared case-insensitively because state files written before 2026-09-23 may carry display case. Prod and closeness still read the item's own task file — SC8 pins both to it. The rendered `next:` line is bounded at 120 characters, matching the `blocked by:` line, because a goal's SC/DoD row runs to hundreds of characters where a task row did not.

## v0.151.2

- docs: **drop the `--stuck-min` hint from `/supervisor:who-needs-my-help`.** The sweep accepts the flag for parity with `who-needs-me` and ignores it, so the UI hint promised a threshold this command does not honour — found by the command's own `/coding:audit-slash-command` re-audit (8/10, no criticals).

## v0.151.1

- fix: **a park is a gate only when the operator could actually clear it.** The sweep handed sessions parked on ordinary assistant prose to the operator, because `gate_class()` returns C — the everything-else bucket, and C is operator-bound — for any park that is not A/B/D/E. A park now counts as a gate only when its closer carries an operator-facing verb (`approve:` / `pick —` / `you run:` / `review:`) **or** the attention store holds an open item for it. The closer is also read from the transcript's own `👤 You:` line rather than falling back to the record's `detail`, which is arbitrary prose rather than an ask. Measured on the first live fleet run: two panes were classified `awaiting-operator` while all three instruments — no open store item, no operator verb, no modal footer — agreed they were not gated. Deriving "no gate" from the *cause* class instead would have dropped the two rows that legitimately gate, since an answered record sends their cause to `unknown` too.

## v0.151.0

- feat: **add `/supervisor:who-needs-my-help` — one pass over every live session: what it is parked on (runbook gate class A–E) and why (cause class), the operator-bound rows handed over ordered prod-first then closest-to-SC with a jump link and a recommended answer, and the cause classes recurring across two or more distinct calendar days.** Report-only on gates: it answers no gate, drives no modal and relays no approval. The one outward action is a `SendMessage` per live manager asking for its next SC/DoD item and what blocks it. The command is a two-step router rather than a print-verbatim one, because `SendMessage` is an agent tool that a script cannot call — step 1 runs `scripts/who-needs-my-help.py` and prints its output, step 2 sends one message to each manager the script names in its `Managers to query` block.

## v0.150.0

- feat: **`stop_agent` — the manager-facing stop for an A2A worker, and the third of the controller's `create` / `list` / `stop` operations to be reachable from the supervisor.** The controller has implemented `stop` since `v0.3.0` and `a2aControlRequestFor({operation:'stop', …})` has built its request correctly since the A2A client shipped, but **no caller invoked it and no tool exposed it** — so a manager could create and list a worker over A2A and had no way to remove one, leaving a Docker container or a k3s pod plus its `Config` CR behind with no manager-facing reap. The new tool resolves the worker id off the worker's own record and calls the controller, so `docker ps -a` and the pod / `Config` CR are both cleared by one manager call. ⚠️ **Keyed on the WORKER ID, never a task name**, and structurally so rather than by a caller's care: the id is a required argument to `a2aStopCallFor` and the builder refuses a `stop` that names a task, because one task can be spawned again after its first worker died and a task-keyed stop would remove the successor. The id comes from the ledger's `worker_id`, restored on rehydrate by `toAgentRecord`. ⚠️ **It is a CONTROL-PLANE call to the controller, authenticated with the controller token — not a turn to the worker.** It therefore needs no worker endpoint and no worker token, which is what lets a manager stop a row this process never spawned, including one re-adopted after a reconnect: those can be seen but not messaged (`resolveMessageChannel` refuses them), and they **can** be stopped. Scoped to the `docker` / `k8s` targets; a tab worker is closed in its tab and a cluster worker is the shared long-lived pod. ⚠️ A `TASK_STATE_FAILED` reply is reported as an **unknown outcome**, not as "still running" — the controller's store-copy failure lands after the executor has run, so a FAILED stop can accompany a worker that was removed, and the caller is told to reconcile against `list` rather than assume.

## v0.149.2

- docs: **the fleet surface and the README now say where the cluster and A2A tokens actually come from, and name the trap that cost a session to find.** `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 7 claimed both values resolve *"from either of two sources, env first: `SUPERVISOR_CLUSTER_URL` / `INTERACTIVE_AUTH_TOKEN` on the server entry, or a `cluster` object in the supervisor config file"* — true when written, false since the migration: the TOKEN has no file source at all now, and `"cluster.token"` is refused rather than read. The README's config block repeated the claim and framed a token in the file as a permissions question (`chmod 600`) rather than an error. ⚠️ **Both files now also record the caching trap as it applies to `command`, not only to an `env` block.** Item 7 already warned that an MCP `env` block is cached at session start; measured 2026-10-14, so is the `command` itself — `ps` showed every supervisor still running `bun run --cwd … start` after its entry had been repointed at `python3 …/scripts/mcp-start.py`, so a Reconnect silently kept the old launcher and resolved no tokens at all. **A changed `command` needs a NEW session.** The A2A pair — `SUPERVISOR_A2A_URL` / `SUPERVISOR_A2A_TOKEN`, previously undocumented in both files — is now stated alongside.

## v0.149.1

- fix: **the manager cap reads `config.json` live, counts a slow board instead of killing it, and names the count failure rather than the limit.** Three defects on one refusal path, each observed 2026-10-14 when `spawn.maxManagers` was raised 5 → 10 and two `role: manager` spawns were still refused against 5. ⚠️ **The config was read once at module load and frozen**, which is right for every key that cannot change under a running server and wrong for the spawn limits — the operator edits those *because* they want the next spawn to differ from the last. `config-reload.mjs` re-reads on an mtime move (one `stat` on the hot path, a parse only when something changed), and a missing file keeps the last good value: editors write via rename, so the path is briefly absent mid-write, and reading that absence as "no config" would drop the operator's limits to the built-in defaults at the exact moment they are editing them. ⚠️ **The count was being killed at 20 s by `RUN_TIMEOUT_MS`, inherited from the cluster reader.** The board walks the vault to classify every registry session, and measured 14–73 s under load (7.1 s on a quiet fleet), so the count failed intermittently — and a killed count is indistinguishable from an uncountable fleet. The count now runs under its own `COUNT_TIMEOUT_MS` (120 s), raised only against a measured wall time; the board's own cost is tracked separately rather than papered over here. ⚠️ **`managerSessions` returned a bare `null` and discarded `stderr`**, so a timeout, a missing interpreter and a Python traceback all reached the operator as the same "the count could not be taken" — beside a limit that was usually the stale one, sending them to the single value that was already correct. It now returns `{ ids, error }`, and the refusal leads with the cause while still naming the limit now in effect.

- fix: **the cluster and A2A refusals named a launcher that no longer exists, and prescribed a remedy that does not work.** Both `resolveAuthToken`'s unset-token message (`server/cluster-spawn.mjs`) and `resolveA2AControllerTarget`'s (`server/a2a-spawn.mjs`) told the operator to *"point the supervisor MCP entry at `scripts/supervisor-launch.sh` and Reconnect"* — a bash wrapper that was written, retired and deleted before the release that shipped the string, so the advice named a file no checkout contains. ⚠️ **The remedy was wrong in a second way, and that one is not cosmetic.** A `/mcp` Reconnect does **not** pick up a changed `command`: Claude Code caches the MCP server definition at session start and re-spawns from that cache, so an operator who followed the instruction exactly would edit the config, Reconnect, and still have no token — measured 2026-10-14, `ps` showing every supervisor still running `bun run --cwd … start` after the entry had been repointed at `python3 …/scripts/mcp-start.py`. Both messages now name `scripts/mcp-start.py` and state that a changed `command` needs a **NEW session**; the env-block note that already said as much is folded in beside it, since it is the same caching rule.

## v0.149.0

- feat: **the supervisor's cluster and A2A tokens come from TeamVault, and a plaintext token in the config file is now REFUSED rather than read.** `scripts/mcp-start.py` — already the launcher the shipped `.mcp.json` names — resolves `INTERACTIVE_AUTH_TOKEN` (TeamVault key `VO0Gmw`) and `SUPERVISOR_A2A_TOKEN` (key `lO4j5q`) with `teamvault-cli` before exec'ing the server, so `server/config.mjs:175,193,210-211` needed no change: it already reads those names env-first. ⚠️ **Resolving them in the EXISTING launcher rather than a second one is the point, and the first attempt at this change got it wrong.** That file already resolves bun and declares `SUPERVISOR_CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, and the shipped `.mcp.json` already names it — so a new bash launcher would have been a second launcher in sequence, reached by nobody on the plugin path, whose user could then configure a token NOWHERE: the plaintext route is refused and nothing set the env. ⚠️ **An unresolved token DEGRADES; it never stops the server, and this is the one place the launcher does not fail closed.** This process gates every supervisor tool, not only the two spawn targets that consume a token, so exiting non-zero on a TeamVault outage would cost a session `list_agents`, `spawn_agent(target="local")` and every manager verb — the whole supervisor, for every session at once. The server already refuses a cluster or A2A spawn with a sentence naming the missing variable, which is the right layer for it. ⚠️ **Only the lookup KEY NAME is ever logged.** A TeamVault lookup key is a short identifier rather than a secret; the value reaches no stream, on the success path or the failure one.
