An explicit value already in the environment is never overwritten, matching `config.mjs`'s env-first precedence. ⚠️ **The plaintext refusal is on PRESENCE, not precedence, and that is the whole point.** `resolveClusterTarget` and `resolveA2AControllerTarget` refuse a set `cluster.token` / `a2a.token` *before* the env-vs-file pick, because env-first means the file value never WINS: a check at the point of use would never see it, and the plaintext token would sit in the file indefinitely while every spawn kept working. A file-sourced token is no longer a source at all — both resolvers' token lists shrink to the env var, and the refusals plus the 401 hint that named `"cluster.token"` now name the launcher. `''` stays absence, so a key emptied rather than deleted does not refuse.

## v0.148.0

- feat: **`who-needs-me` now says WHY a pane is parked, not only that it is.** Every `Needs you` row leads with a cause class — `awaiting-operator`, `waiting-on-task <name>`, `waiting-on-clock <date>`, `cap-blocked`, `unknown` — and a new `Board card missing` section lists the parked panes the attention board carries no card for. ⚠️ **The cause enum is a SECOND AXIS, not a new value in the runbook's A–E gate triage classes.** A–E says who may CLEAR a gate; this says why the pane is PARKED, and the two co-occur on one pane — a pane can be class D and `board-card-expired` at once — so folding either into the other would drop a dimension rather than simplify. ⚠️ **The uncarded gates get their OWN section rather than joining `Needs you`, and that is the design rather than a convenience.** `blocked` is built from the store's own items, so any pane in it has a card *by construction* — the flags could never fire from inside it. Folding the event-log rows in instead would re-admit the dead-asker rows the store read exists to eliminate (measured 2026-09-28, pane 2555: a session blocked on a gate writes nothing, its transcript ages past `LIVE_WINDOW`, and the reader calls a live gate `provably finished`), so `Needs you` stays store-only and its count is unchanged; the new section re-checks liveness per row, where a mis-judged row costs one visible line instead of a wrong block count. ⚠️ **A failed store read emits NO flag.** `StoreUnreachable` must not collapse to "no card", or an outage marks every parked pane `board-card-missing` — the `docs/pane-reads.md` rule ("Return `None` on failure — never `{}` / `[]` / `0`"), which is why `STORE_OK` is a tri-state and not a boolean default.

- feat: **a recurring-cause tally at `~/.claude/state/who-needs-me/causes.json` flags a cause class seen on 3 or more distinct DAYS with no owning task.** One run is one invocation, deduped per calendar day, so the attention watcher's polling cannot inflate the count — without the dedup, three sweeps of one afternoon would read as recurring. ⚠️ **Owner resolution is opt-in (`--resolve-owners`), never on the render path:** `vault-cli task search` loads a sentence-transformer model (~10 s) and writes its INFO logs to stdout *as JSON array elements*, so results and log lines interleave — resolving owners inline would make this command slow AND its output unparseable. ⚠️ **The command's contract changes with it.** `commands/who-needs-me.md` said "Read-only."; it now says "Read-only on the fleet; writes its own cause tally", because the read-only promise was always about not mutating the fleet — no spawns, kills or messages — and that is unchanged.

## v0.147.0

- feat: **the fleet round speaks every operator ask, once, as a count and a pointer — and keeps the detail in the chat.** `commands/steps/voice-notify.md` gains a *"Speak the asks; keep the detail in chat"* section: speak every item that needs the operator; say `<N> items need your attention, the links are in the chat` and nothing else — never details, never URLs, never a pane or session list; one `mcp__tts__say` call per round however many items it names, never N calls for N items; details and jump links in the chat in the same turn; silence when nothing needs the operator. ⚠️ **The composition is written down rather than left to the reader, because four existing rules already govern this surface.** `~/.claude/hooks/voice-mode.py` decides *whether* voice is on — these rules shape only what it says, and never resurrect voice over an explicit `/tts-mcp:off`; `commands/steps/rules.md` already carries the silence test (*"Silence is the default; the test is an ACTION, not a finding"*) and is cited rather than restated; `docs/voice-asks.md` pairs a `?`-bearing `say` with a board card, and the count-and-pointer line carries no `?` so it mints none — the gates reach the board through the `notify-gate.py --layer fleet` publish that was already there; and the global *"Attention Costs The Operator"* / *"Voice Chat Detail On Request"* rules are the same test one level up. ⚠️ **Measured, not assumed:** the operator's own Fleet Manager session (64b4a415) made 23 `mcp__tts__say` calls on 2026-10-14 — eleven in the old detailed shape before the instruction, twelve in the count-and-pointer shape after it — so this section encodes a shape already proven in a live session, which until now lived only in that session's ledger.

## v0.146.0

- feat: **`list_agents` carries every worker's open attention cards, so a manager sees a permission prompt on a worker it did NOT spawn — and a foreign `answer_permission` names the owner instead of refusing anonymously.** Both gaps were in `server/supervisor.mjs`, and both were the same shape: an answer that was true of *this* process and silent about the rest of the fleet. ⚠️ **`list_agents` reads the store ONCE for the whole roster, never once per worker.** A `fetchOpenCards` per agent would be N round-trips against a 3 s timeout each, in front of every manager sweep — so with the store down the roster read would take N × 3 s and stop being a roster read at all. The new `fetchAttentionItems` (`server/open-cards.mjs`) fetches the list once and the already-pure `openCardsOf` does the per-session filter, which is also why the new export is the fetch and not a second filter. ⚠️ **`null` stays distinct from `[]` on every row.** `null` is "the store could not be read"; `[]` is "read, and this worker holds nothing open". Collapsing them reports a parked worker as unblocked whenever the store is down — the exact false negative this field exists to remove, and the reason the null check is written at the call site rather than left to `openCardsOf`, which would answer `[]` for an unreadable store.

- fix: **a foreign `answer_permission` now names the worker, its spawning session, and the route that settles it — instead of `no pending permission <id> (already answered, or expired)`, which named neither.** `pending` is one server process's Map, so a park another manager's server holds is not in it; the old text left the one caller who could act with nothing to act on. ⚠️ **The refusal is a read rather than a field lookup, and that is the design constraint rather than a shortcut.** A store card carries `producer_id` but no request id (`Attention Permission Answer Path` § The join — carrying the route in `payload` was rejected, not merely dispreferred), so a request id cannot address a card directly. A worker's ledger row carries BOTH halves — its own `session_id`, which IS the card's `producer_id`, and `spawner_session_id` — and the park rides that row as `parked_permission.request_id`. `server/park-refusal.mjs` owns the join and the text, with the ledger reader injected so the lookup is testable without a store. ⚠️ **A row that cannot be found still gets the route**: an unreadable ledger and a request already settled here are not distinguishable at that point, and the route is the right advice for both — never a bare "no such permission".

## v0.145.3

- docs: **SC2's per-file probe now reads 0 on every one of the fifteen files it names — the three remaining hits were comments, not code.** The file-write retirement's own prose spelled the retired path in `server/ledger.mjs`, and the retired path and variable in `server/config.mjs`, and SC2 asserts each accessor with a pinned `git grep -nE 'claude-supervisor/sessions|SUPERVISOR_LEDGER_DIR'` that cannot tell a comment from a call — so a file whose code was already clean still failed the criterion. Both comments are reworded to spell neither literal and now point at `docs/fleet-surface.md`, which carries the operator-facing note. ⚠️ **No code changed** — the previous increment deleted every writer and reader, and this is the criterion it is asserted against. ⚠️ **The process failure is the part worth keeping:** the probe IS the acceptance criterion for the increment, and it was run as a post-merge confirmation instead of as a pre-PR gate, so it failed on `master` rather than on the branch. Run the criterion the increment exists to satisfy BEFORE opening its PR, not after merging it.

## v0.145.2

- fix: **the durable spawn ledger is attention-controller's store ALONE — the file write is retired with its last reader, and `config.ledgerDir` and `SUPERVISOR_LEDGER_DIR` go with it.** `server/supervisor.mjs`'s two write sites (`updateRecord` at `:665`, `writeRecord` at `:722`) are gone, so a spawn no longer creates anything under `$XDG_STATE_HOME/claude-supervisor/sessions` — which is what SC1 asserts. ⚠️ **The merge source moved from the file to the record this process already holds.** The patch arm used to re-read the file and merge into it, which made the endpoint row a copy of a copy: a vanished or rotated file froze that row silently, and two in-flight patches could race in the read-modify-write window. The record the create arm built is held on the agent (`agent.ledgerRecord`) and merged through a new pure `mergeRecord(current, patch)` in `server/ledger.mjs`, so there is one source for this process's own row and no round trip. ⚠️ **The park depends on this, which is why it is not a tidy-up.** A park is written when a gate opens and the result when the turn ends; merging the PATCH alone rather than the whole row would drop the park in between, and a resumed worker would then carry no decision — silently, since the resume path refuses a decision it cannot tie to a park. ⚠️ **The create arm is pinned as running FIRST for every mode** — headless on the SDK's `init` message (`agent-loop.mjs:58`), and cluster / a2a / interactive inside their own spawn paths — so a patch that finds no record is a session id that resolved with no spawn behind it, which is the case the existing guard already reports. ⚠️ **`ledger.mjs` no longer reads or writes anything:** `recordPath`, `writeRecord`, `readRecord`, `updateRecord` and `listRecords` are DELETED rather than left unused, because a dead writer is how a later reader gets re-wired to a store nobody maintains. ⚠️ **`publishLedgerRow` is now the only durable write, and that is a change in kind rather than in address.** A missed POST used to cost a second copy; it now costs the record, which is why the shutdown path's wait for in-flight publishes matters more than it did.

- test: **the merge property moved one layer in rather than being dropped with the file.** The file-era tests that exercised "a completion patch merges rather than replacing the spawn fields" ran through `writeRecord`/`updateRecord`; they run against `mergeRecord` now, and three cases are new because the property is load-bearing in a way it was not: a park survives a later `{result}` patch, clearing a park is an explicit `null` rather than an omitted key (a spread cannot express a deletion), and merging into no record answers `null` rather than inventing a row missing the spawn edge. ⚠️ **Four call-site pins replace the two negative ones in `registry-rehydrate.test.mjs`** — `config.ledgerDir` and the two file helpers must appear nowhere in `supervisor.mjs`, and both the merge call and the `agent.ledgerRecord` assignment that keeps the record must still be there. ⚠️ The pins are the point rather than belt-and-braces: nothing else in this repo exercises the spawn path's durable half, so a revert that re-added the file write would leave every behavioural test green.

## v0.145.1

- fix: **the supervisor's two remaining spawn-ledger READS go through attention-controller's endpoint, so `config.ledgerDir` no longer answers any read in the server.** `spawnAgent`'s decision-on-resume park read and the A2A liveness mirror were the last two file readers. A new `readSessionLedgerRow(sessionId)` in `server/session-ledger.mjs` serves the first — the per-id route, URL-encoded — and the existing `readSessionLedger()` serves the second. ⚠️ **The park needed no field rename, and that was CHECKED rather than assumed:** `PARK_FIELD` is `parked_permission`, spelled identically on the wire and in the file record, so the one field this path depends on is the one field the two vocabularies agree on. ⚠️ **`readSessionLedgerRow` answers `null` for BOTH "no such row" and "could not read it", deliberately matching the reader it replaces** — `ledger.mjs`'s `readRecord` catches every failure and answers `null`, so a caller written against it already treats the two as one answer. Widening that here would smuggle a behaviour change in under a read swap; the one caller refuses on `null`, which is the SAFE direction for both readings, since it declines to carry a decision it cannot tie to a park. ⚠️ **The A2A mirror's filter transfers UNCHANGED** — `readSessionLedger` returns the store's wire rows verbatim and `mode`, `worker_id`, `ended_at` are spelled identically on both sides — and its `null` becomes `[]`, exactly what `listRecords` did for a directory it could not read: the tick is skipped and the next one retries, which is safe because the poll is idempotent. ⚠️ **The two WRITE sites at `supervisor.mjs:665` and `:722` are deliberately NOT in this change.** They are the file-write retirement, which is the step after this one, and SC1 stays red until it lands.

- test: **nine behavioural cases for `readSessionLedgerRow`** — a 200 with an object is the row; the read goes to the per-id route URL-encoded; a 404 and a 500 are `null`; a throwing transport, a non-object body and a non-JSON body are `null`; an empty or absent id and an explicitly-off endpoint dial nothing. **Plus two call-site pins** in the wiring test that already reads `supervisor.mjs`'s text, because the switch itself is otherwise invisible — every behavioural test would stay green if `readRecord(config.ledgerDir, resume)` came back. ⚠️ The pins deliberately do NOT assert the write sites' removal; those are still the file's on this tree.

## v0.145.0

- fix: **the last five `scripts/` readers of the spawn ledger read it from the attention store's endpoint, so every Python accessor SC2 names is off the file tree.** `fleet-sessions.py`, `check-spawn-ledger.py`, `stuck-heal.py`, `ledger-drill.py` and `shutdown-drill.py` each kept a reader of their own; all five delegate to `spawn-ledger.py` now, which is the single endpoint client. ⚠️ **Each caller keeps its OWN failure answer, and the differences are deliberate rather than leftovers.** `fleet-sessions.py` and `check-spawn-ledger.py` collapse an unreadable store and an empty one into `{}` — a roster must still render, and a checker reports rather than decides — and `stuck-heal.py` collapses them for the same reason in a sharper form: its zero means "resume headless", the recoverable rung, so refusing there would skip a first resume rather than repeat one. `adopt-orphans.py` is the one that must NOT collapse, and it calls `load_ledger_checked` for that reason. ⚠️ **The two DRILLS changed shape rather than only address.** `ledger-drill.py` and `shutdown-drill.py` proved the durable ledger by watching `<session_id>.json` files appear; they watch the store's rows appear now, which is the same claim about the same record — and the endpoint is what every reader consults, so a drill still watching the file would prove a store nothing reads. ⚠️ **Both drills now treat an unreadable store at the START as fatal**, where the file version could not tell "no store" from "no rows yet": with an empty baseline every pre-existing row reads as new, and the drill would pass against a store it never read. `check-spawn-ledger.py` and `stuck-heal.py` take `--endpoint` now, and `stuck-heal.py` keeps `--ledger-dir` accepted-and-ignored with a warning — the same call `worker-sessions.py` already made; `fleet-sessions.py` and the two drills resolve the store from `$ATTENTION_STORE_URL` alone.

- test: **the five suites moved their ledger fixtures onto `FixtureEndpoint`, and two of them are the evidence that the fixture is load-bearing rather than decoration.** `test_check_spawn_ledger.py`'s `test_config_sourced_new_worker_fails` is the case that must FAIL — against an empty store **every** case in that file reports OK, so a no-op swap would have left the suite green while it checked nothing. `test_stuck_heal.py`'s counts all read **0** against an empty store, and 0 is the value that maps to "resume headless", so the ladder would have appeared to work while never leaving its first rung. `test_fleet_sessions.py`'s `LedgerDirResolution` became `LedgerSourceResolution` and gained the retirement case: a caller setting the old ledger-directory variable must not believe it redirected the read.

## v0.144.2

- fix: **the gate-owner filter and the orphan adopter read the spawn ledger from the attention store's endpoint, so ownership resolves from the store every manager writes to rather than from this machine's file tree.** `gate-owner-filter.py` delegates to `spawn-ledger.py`'s reader instead of globbing `<session_id>.json` — the second of the five modules that had kept a reader of its own — and `adopt-orphans.py` reaches the store TRANSITIVELY through it, deliberately gaining no endpoint client of its own: the same one-reader rule `session-liveness.py` already keeps. ⚠️ **The two callers need OPPOSITE answers from one read, and that is what the new `load_ledger_checked` exists for.** The filter must fail OPEN — an unreadable ledger resolves every spawner as a manager, and keeping every gate is the safe direction there — so `load_ledger` deliberately collapses an unreadable store and an empty one into `{}`. The adopter must REFUSE instead: adopting on spawn edges it never read would hand one manager's workers to another, so it calls the checked variant and exits 1 on `None`, while a store that IS readable and empty still exits 0 because that one is a real answer. ⚠️ **The spawn edge is read under BOTH names** — `spawner_session_id` on the wire, `parent_session` in the file record — matching `server/registry-rehydrate.mjs`'s `toAgentRecord`, which made the same move. Asking for one name alone returns `None` rather than an error, and `None` there is not a missing field: it is the "no spawner" answer that decides whether a gate is emitted at all, so a wrong name would silently drop the whole fleet's gates. `--ledger-dir` is accepted-and-ignored with a warning on both scripts, and `--endpoint` is the new override.

- test: **`endpoint_fixture.py` gained `add_ledger_row`, so a suite can open the store once in `setUp` and have each case plant its own rows.** Both gate-path suites moved their ledger fixtures off the filesystem. ⚠️ One of them needed the isolated store rather than treating the swap as a no-op: its file-era spelling pointed `--ledger-dir` at a path that did not exist, which read as an empty ledger, so switching it to the endpoint without a fixture would have made those cases read the MACHINE's fleet and their verdicts would have depended on whoever happened to be running. The unreadable-store arm is now driven by a 500 on the ledger route rather than a chmodded directory, so it exercises what the reader actually consults — and it FAILED before the fixture was extended, which is what makes it discriminating rather than merely green.

## v0.144.1

- fix: **§3's "check whether the answer already exists" lever now names the search space, not just the act of searching.** The lever told a session to look for evidence already on disk; it did not say *where*, and one window can hold only half the discriminator — a `200` is ambiguous under quota, while the `429` beside it is not. Observed 2026-10-08: a run read the prod proxy log, saw only `200`s, and published *"quota is disproven; inbound filters are the dropper"* to the Fleet Manager, while the owning task had recorded the same `200` **and the `429`s** three weeks earlier and read it correctly. The retraction cost a handoff and a peer task built on the wrong premise.

## v0.144.0

- fix: **every `scripts/` reader of the spawn ledger now reads the attention store's endpoint, so the fleet-wide worker cap sees the whole ledger rather than this machine's file tree.** `spawn-ledger.py` — the one module `worker-sessions.py`, `session-liveness.py` and `adopt-orphans.py` reach the ledger through, so switching it satisfies four of the named accessors at once — asks `GET /api/1.0/session-ledger` instead of walking `<session_id>.json` files, and the three-state contract is unchanged: an empty store is `{}` ("nobody has ever been spawned"), an unreadable one is `None`, and the two must never collapse, because `None` REFUSES a spawn and `{}` permits one. ⚠️ **A 404 is `None`, and that is the deploy-order hazard made explicit rather than silent:** a controller older than the session-ledger route answers 404, which the cap reads as "count could not be taken", where an empty store permits a spawn. ⚠️ **The retired `SUPERVISOR_LEDGER_DIR` override is dropped rather than honoured.** The ledger now lives in the attention store beside the heartbeat, so `$ATTENTION_STORE_URL` is what points at it; honouring the old directory variable would be worse than removing it, because a caller that set it would believe it had redirected a read that still went to the endpoint. `worker-sessions.py` keeps `--ledger-dir` as accepted-and-ignored with a warning, the same call it already made for `--dir` and `--heartbeat-dir`.

- test: **`endpoint_fixture.py` now serves the session-ledger routes beside the heartbeat's, and the two suites that planted a ledger directory plant rows instead.** The ledger was a directory of files until this change, so a suite that still planted one would have asserted against a ledger the reader never opens — passing on a counter that saw nothing at all, which is the confident-empty-fleet shape these suites exist to refuse. The two directory-shaped cases became store-shaped ones rather than being deleted, and one is new: an unreadable store (500) and a **missing route (404)** are the answers that REFUSE a spawn, and they must stay distinct from an empty store, which permits one.

## v0.143.0

- fix: **the plugin now declares the server's Claude home, so it binds under any profile rather than only `~/.claude`.** `server/config.mjs` refuses to boot when the inherited `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` disagrees with the server's resolved Claude home — deliberately, because such a server reads its session registry and transcript root from a place no fleet reader looks — and the escape hatch that refusal names (`SUPERVISOR_CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`) was never set by the shipped `.mcp.json`, so a clean `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `--config-dir`, CI, or any other machine's home was refused by construction. Measured 2026-10-14 on v0.142.0: `claude mcp list` under a fresh profile reported `✘ Failed to connect — CONNECTION_CLOSED`, the client's MCP log carrying the server's own stderr refusal. ⚠️ The declaration is made by a new `scripts/mcp-start.py` rather than as an `env` block in `.mcp.json`, because Claude Code substitutes an *unset* `${VAR}` reference with the LITERAL text (measured 2026-10-14: `[prefix-${UNSET}-suffix]` arrived verbatim, beside a `Missing environment variables` warning) — so `"env": {"SUPERVISOR_CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR": "${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR}"}` would hand the server the string `${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR}` as its home on any machine that never sets the variable, the exact silent-wrong-home defect the refusal exists to prevent. Declared only when the ambient value is set, and never over an explicit `SUPERVISOR_CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`. ⚠️ The launcher also resolves `bun` through `$BUN_INSTALL/bin`, `~/.bun/bin` and `PATH` instead of assuming it on `PATH`, which closes the `ENOENT` the install runbook documents for a session launched outside a terminal.

- test: the launcher declares the ambient config dir as the server's home, leaves an unset one unset, never overwrites an explicit declaration, resolves bun in the documented order, and reports a missing bun on stderr instead of exec'ing.

## v0.142.1

- fix: **an A2A spawn on k8s now waits for the worker behind the ingress, instead of orphaning it on the ingress's own 404.** Measured 2026-10-14 in the first `spawn_agent(target: 'k8s')` against the nuke-dev controller: the card answered **404** the instant `create` returned (no route while the pod was Pending), **503** once the route existed, and **200** about 45 s after create. The v0.140.2 readiness retry treated any HTTP answer as the worker speaking, so the spawn errored and the worker was orphaned. `fetchWorkerCard` now also retries 404 / 502 / 503 / 504, and the deadline rises from 30 s to **120 s** to cover pod scheduling and start. Any other HTTP answer stays final, and a genuine 404 still fails once the deadline passes.

- test: an ingress 404 then 503 is retried until the worker answers; a 404 past the deadline still refuses; a 401 is final and never retried.

## v0.142.0

- fix: **the fleet-wide worker cap counts from the session-ledger endpoint, so it reads one store rather than a file tree.** `readLedger` was the last server-side reader of `~/.local/state/claude-supervisor/sessions/`; it now reads the endpoint and keeps its three-state contract exactly — an empty store is `{}` ("nobody has ever been spawned"), a store that cannot be read is `null`, and the two must never collapse, because `null` refuses a spawn and `[]` permits one. ⚠️ **Deploy precondition, and it is not optional:** the endpoint holds only the rows written since `writeLedger` began publishing to it, so switching this reader before SC5's one-time import lands makes the cap UNDER-count a live worker spawned before the migration — the direction that permits an over-spawn. Import first, then deploy.

- fix: **`list_agents` reads the roster at REQUEST time, so a worker another manager spawned — or one resumed from a WezTerm pane — is visible without a server restart.** The roster was a BOOT-TIME SNAPSHOT: `rehydratableAgents()` adopted once at import and `pruneRehydratedAgents()` could only remove, so a session that was not in `live` at that instant stayed invisible to that process for its whole life, however live it became. Measured 2026-10-13: **4 of 35** live-and-ledgered sessions were absent from a live roster, and the cleanest proof is that the measuring session's OWN id was one of them — its heartbeat is written by the very server that performs the adoption, so at import it cannot yet exist. The ledger row was correct and present in all four cases; nothing was missing and nothing collided. New `server/session-ledger.mjs` reads the spawn ledger from attention-controller's endpoint (identity only — liveness stays a separate read, joined on `session_id`, the same split the file path kept), and `currentRoster()` unions it with this process's own in-process workers, deduping on `session_id` with the in-process row winning because it alone carries the live transcript, park queue and permission rules. `list_agents`, `agent_status` and `send_agent_message` all read that roster, and `registry-rehydrate.mjs`'s adoption rule — live-only, and `agent_id` DISAMBIGUATED rather than dropped — is unchanged and now has one implementation (`agentsFromRecords`) shared by the request-time and boot-time paths, so the two cannot answer differently. ⚠️ **`writeLedger` publishes each row to the endpoint BESIDE the file write, and that dual-write is transitional and deliberate:** the roster reads the endpoint, so a spawn that wrote only a file would be invisible to every manager, while the file is still what the resume path and the shutdown stamp read. Retiring the file is a separate step with its own acceptance. The POST is fire-and-forget on purpose — a store outage must not turn a spawn that already produced a working worker into a failed one — **but the SHUTDOWN path now waits for the writes already in flight**, because `process.exit(0)` in the same tick killed every one of them deterministically (`fetch` cannot complete a round trip synchronously) and the shutdown stamp is the one write whose whole purpose is to stop a record asserting `running` forever. ⚠️ **And a foreign row's roster id is now a function of the ROW ALONE** (`agent_1~<sid8>`), where deriving it from this process's own Map made the same row answer under two ids across two calls — so a manager that read `list_agents` and then called `send_agent_message` with an id from it reached whichever worker happened to hold that id, silently and in the wrong pane; `agent_1` is the common case, claimed by 215 of the 1891 ledger rows. ⚠️ The union itself moved out of `currentRoster` into an exported `unionRoster` so it can be tested behaviourally: it lived in `supervisor.mjs`, which cannot be imported, so the heart of this change was pinned only by regexes over source text — which assert a source's shape, never its behaviour. ⚠️ `null` still means "could not read it" from either store, never an empty fleet. ⚠️ **`writeLedger` now REFUSES an adopted row outright, which is what makes the single-writer guarantee structural rather than a discipline each call site must remember.** A rehydrated row's record belongs to the server that spawned that worker, and since the endpoint's `Post` REPLACES a whole row rather than merging a patch, a write from a second server would not lose one field — it would rebuild the record from a row carrying identity alone and blank everything the owner wrote. ⚠️ **That path is reachable, not hypothetical:** `send_agent_message` looks its agent up in `currentRoster()` — which is the point, since the worker a manager messages is the one it did not spawn — and the cluster arm of that handler writes `{ result }` onto it, so before this guard a manager messaging another server's cluster worker erased that worker's row. The refusal is logged, because that turn's result genuinely goes unrecorded for a worker this process does not own and the loss must be readable rather than discovered.

- feat: **an A2A `create` call can now write the exact body it sends, at the send site, when `a2a.captureDir` is set in the supervisor config.** This is SC2's evidence — a `docker` and a `k8s` spawn must send byte-identical calls apart from per-worker values, and that can only be shown by capturing what was actually sent. Off by default; one JSON file per call (`<at>-<target>-create.json`, carrying `at`, `target`, `task`, `url`, `body`). ⚠️ The hook is handed the URL and the body only, never the headers, because the bearer token travels in `Authorization`. A capture that throws never fails the spawn.

- test: the capture receives exactly the body sent and never the token; a throwing capture leaves the spawn intact.

## v0.141.0

- feat: **`send_agent_message` now reaches an A2A worker: one more turn goes to the WORKER's own endpoint, on the conversation its first turn opened, and the controller is not in the path.** Before this, a `docker` / `k8s` worker fell through to the headless refusal, so no follow-up turn could reach it at all. The send is awaited and the collection is not, exactly as for the first turn, and the reply carries `send_ms` — this server's own measurement of the send, so a latency claim does not have to be read off a caller's round trip. The conversation id is recorded on the in-memory agent record after the first send, beside the token. ⚠️ A row re-adopted after a reconnect holds no token (the ledger never stores one) and is refused rather than sent unauthenticated.

- test: three `resolveMessageChannel` cases — an A2A worker resolves to its endpoint, token and conversation; a token-less re-adopted row is refused; a worker with no first turn has no conversation to continue.

## v0.140.2

- fix: **an A2A spawn now waits for the new worker to listen before reading its Agent Card, instead of losing the worker to a startup race.** `create` returns once the container is *started*, not once its server listens. Measured 2026-10-13 in the second live `spawn_agent(target: 'docker')` against `a2a-worker-controller` v0.4.4: the card fetched the instant `create` returned failed with `fetch failed`, and the same URL answered `200` five seconds later, so the spawn errored and the worker was orphaned. `fetchWorkerCard` now retries a **refused connection** every 500 ms for up to 30 s. An HTTP answer, a 404 included, is the worker speaking and stays final.

- test: three new cases — a refused fetch retried until the worker listens, a worker that never listens giving up at its deadline, and an HTTP answer never being retried.

- fix: **an A2A turn's result is now read from the event shape the SDK actually sends, so a completed turn reports COMPLETED and its reply.** a2a-go v2.6.0 nests a status update's state at `statusUpdate.status.state` and an artifact's parts at `artifactUpdate.artifact.parts`; the collector read both one level too shallow. Measured 2026-10-14 in the first fully successful live `spawn_agent(target: 'docker')`: the worker logged `turn start` and `turn end` 6 s apart, and the supervisor filed the turn as `TASK_STATE_SUBMITTED` with an empty reply. The control-reply parser read a streamed status with the same flat shape and is fixed too. The flat shape is still accepted.

- test: the SDK's nested event shape must yield `COMPLETED` and the reply, and a streamed control reply must read `FAILED` from the nested status — both fail on the pre-fix code.

## v0.140.1

- fix: **`make precommit` now names a missing `server/node_modules` instead of reporting it as three broken tests.** With that directory absent, `node --test` emits one `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` per module it cannot load — `undici` for `cluster-spawn.test.mjs`; the Agent SDK for `mode.test.mjs` and, through the dynamic `await import('./mode.mjs')` at `shipping-settings.test.mjs:45`, for that file too — and then aborts before running a single test. Three module errors read as three broken tests, which is how they were read on 2026-10-14: a worker concluded `origin/master` was red and filed a task against a repo that was green. A `check-server-deps` guard now runs as a prerequisite of `test`, so `make test` and `make precommit` alike print the install command and its directory and stop before the suite. ⚠️ It installs nothing, deliberately: a check that reaches the network fails for reasons that are not about the tree, and an `npm install` there would drop an untracked `server/package-lock.json` into every worktree that ran it. The `Makefile`'s own prerequisite note is unchanged — a comment is not what `make` prints.
- fix: **the remaining vault folder literals in `commands/` are marked, so the plugin no longer names a folder a clean install may not have.** `commands/manager-drive.md` carried the *identical* subject-argument sentence to `commands/manager-loop.md` — which already carried the marker — so one file was edited and its sibling missed, the drift class `docs/fleet-surface.md` warns about. The marker is added at `commands/manager-drive.md`, `commands/steps/manager-resolve.md` (the branch probe, the header example, `Step G`, and the `topics_dir` fallback) and `commands/manager-verify.md`'s two probes. `scripts/budget-baseline.json`'s `commands/manager-drive.md` entry is raised to the size this PR leaves. ⚠️ **Three sites are deliberately left unmarked, each for its own reason:** `commands/manager-verify.md` already **declares** its literals a known defect parked in a separate task; `commands/open.md`'s `topics_dir` is **config-resolved** with vault-cli's own default; and `commands/worker-drive.md`'s is a **provenance citation** of a past measurement rather than a path the command resolves.

_Releases older than v0.140.0 (v0.102.4 … v0.140.0) are archived in [CHANGELOG-archive.md](CHANGELOG-archive.md)._
